=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer sequence of length N: (A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N). There is also a sequence S, which is initially empty.
For each i = 1, 2, \ldots, N in this order, you perform exactly one of the following two operations:

- Append A_i as an element to the end of S.
- Delete the last element of S. You cannot choose this operation if S is empty.

Print the maximum possible value of the sum of the elements of S after all operations.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- -10^9 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

6
3 -1 -4 5 -9 2

Sample Output 1

8

Starting from the initial state where S is an empty sequence, consider the following operations:

- For i = 1, append A_1 = 3 to the end of S. Now, S = (3).
- For i = 2, append A_2 = -1 to the end of S. Now, S = (3, -1).
- For i = 3, delete the last element of S. Now, S = (3).
- For i = 4, append A_4 = 5 to the end of S. Now, S = (3, 5).
- For i = 5, append A_5 = -9 to the end of S. Now, S = (3, 5, -9).
- For i = 6, delete the last element of S. Now, S = (3, 5).

Here, the sum of the elements of S after all operations is 3 + 5 = 8, which is the maximum possible value.

Sample Input 2

1
-1

Sample Output 2

-1

Note that if S is empty, you must choose to append an element.

Sample Input 3

20
-14 74 -48 38 -51 43 5 37 -39 -29 80 -44 -55 59 17 89 -37 -68 38 -16

Sample Output 3

369

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, is the reduction fully correct? Need be careful: The final stack elements are exactly the unmatched pushes. We assumed all non-final indices are matched pushes/pops with net zero in gaps. But is it possible that a non-final index is used as a pop that removes a final element and later that final element is re-pushed? No, final elements are never popped; if popped, not final. The final stack after all operations is set of pushes not popped. We choose a subsequence of push times that are not popped. All other push times are popped. The sequence of stack heights over time is a walk. The times of unmatched pushes (final elements) are those +1 steps that are never matched by a -1. In a Dyck path decomposition, unmatched +1 steps form the final "spine". The matched +1 steps are in pairs with -1 steps; due to LIFO, each matched pair corresponds to an excursion above some spine node. The spine indices indeed have parity constraints as above. Let's prove via stack height h_t = size after t operations. Let final spine pushes occur at times i_j. Between spine pushes, h_t must return to j? Actually after pushing i_j, h = j. Before pushing i_{j+1}, h must be j (since no spine pushes in between and no pops below j; all matched pushes above j popped). Thus h at time i_j -1? At time just before i_{j+1}, h=j. Then push at i_{j+1} increases to j+1. So over interval (i_j, i_{j+1}) inclusive? Let's compute parity. The number of operations between i_j and i_{j+1} excluding i_j but including? Let's define after i_j height j. Before i_{j+1} height j. The operations at times i_j+1 ... i_{j+1}-1 net 0, so length even. Thus i_{j+1}-i_j-1 even. Good. Let operations define a sequence of stack sizes h_t. Let h_0=0, h_N=h. Each step +1 (push) or -1 (pop). Let P be set of push times not popped (final elements). Now, let's think if there is any nuance about "Append A_i as an element to the end of S" vs "Delete the last element of S" exactly one operation per i. We used every index as step. Good.

Now, final code:
import sys
def main():
    data=list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data: return
    N=data[0]
    A=data[1:]
    NEG=-10**30
    dp_odd=NEG
    dp_even=NEG
    for i,x in enumerate(A, start=1):
        if i & 1:
            # odd position
            # dp_odd = max(dp_odd, x, dp_even + x)
            if dp_even != NEG:
                val = dp_even + x
                if val > dp_odd: dp_odd = val
            if x > dp_odd: dp_odd = x
            # Wait if dp_odd initially NEG, x > NEG yes.
        else:
            # even position
            if dp_odd != NEG:
                val = dp_odd + x
                if val > dp_even: dp_even = val
    if N & 1:
        ans = dp_odd
    else:
        ans = dp_even
        if ans < 0: ans = 0
    print(ans)
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

But need be careful: In odd transition, dp_even + x could be computed even if dp_even is NEG; NEG + x still very negative, not > dp_odd maybe if dp_odd is NEG? For first odd i, dp_odd=NEG, dp_even=NEG, NEG+x = -1e30 + x, which is less than NEG? Actually -1e30 + 1e9 = -999999999999999999999? That's greater than -1e30? Wait -10^30 + 10^9 = -999,999,999,999,999,999,999,999,999,? Let's calculate: -1,000,000,000,000,000,000,000,000,000,000 + 1,000,000,000 = -999,999,999,999,999,999,999,000,000,000? Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp_odd" and "dp_even" names maybe confusing but code fine.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that the DP should be "dp[0] = max sum ending at odd, dp[1] = max sum ending at even" and at odd i, "dp[0] = max(dp[0], dp[1] + A_i, A_i)" and at even i, "dp[1] = max(dp[1], dp[0] + A_i)". This is exactly. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "the final stack elements are the unmatched pushes" and "unmatched pushes in a path of length N with final height H have positions i_d satisfying i_d ≡ d mod 2? Let's derive: i_1 odd, i_2 even, i_3 odd, so i_d ≡ d mod 2? If d=1 odd, d=2 even, yes i_d parity = d mod2. Then final height H parity = i_H parity = H mod2. Since total N parity = H mod2. Thus i_H parity = N mod2. Good. This is another way. Our DP selects length h with last parity h mod2; answer ensures h parity N. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any issue with "selected indices parity pattern starts odd and alternates" exactly i_d parity = d mod2. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden problem with "A_i" values and "N" maybe 2e5, but if using "max" with NEG and guard, no issue. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that the answer for N even could be dp_even but dp_even corresponds to a subsequence of length 0? No, dp_even only updated by appending to dp_odd, so length at least2? Actually dp_even at i2 from dp_odd at i1 length1 + i2 length2. So length even. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp_odd" at i3 from dp_even at i2 length2 + i3 length3, or from x length1. So dp_odd can be length1 or3 etc. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any hidden problem with "the maximum possible value" if there are multiple optimal operation sequences; no.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "S" initially empty and "append A_i as an element to the end of S" if S is empty and A_i negative; must append. Our DP for N odd all negative chooses a negative at some odd index, but what about the forced pushes before that odd index? They are temp and popped, so final sum only selected negative. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that for N odd, final stack could have height >1 but sum greater than single negative; DP considers. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "the sum of the elements of S after all operations" if S has elements but sum0; yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp_odd = max(dp_odd, dp_even + x)" if dp_even valid but dp_even + x is less than NEG? no.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that the DP should process "delete" operations as allowing "skip" of an element without changing selected subsequence; our parity characterization accounts for skips as temp operations. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "the sequence S, which is initially empty" and "Delete the last element of S. You cannot choose this operation if S is empty." If we choose delete at a time when S has elements, we remove the last element; this could be a selected final element if we don't plan properly. Our DP only selects subsequences that can be realized with no selected element popped. Good.

Actually NEG+x < NEG, so no invalid update. But if x positive, invalid. Guard robust. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp_odd = max(dp_odd, x)" if x is NEG? A_i min -1e9, not NEG. Good.

No, selected elements are final stack; height equals count. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that a pop operation could remove a temp element that was pushed before a selected element? No, because selected element would be above it; cannot pop below selected. Our construction avoids. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any hidden problem with "N" up to 2e5 and "A_i" up to 1e9, but if using "NEG=-10**30" and "dp_even != NEG" exact equality; if dp_even is NEG and x is negative, NEG+x would be less than NEG, so even if not guarded, max wouldn't choose it? Actually NEG+x < NEG, so no invalid update. But if x positive, invalid. Guard robust. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp_odd = max(dp_odd, x)" if x is NEG? A_i min -1e9, not NEG. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that the answer for N odd should be dp_odd but if dp_odd is less than 0 and N odd, maybe we could choose to pop all elements and then have empty? No. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "the final stack elements are the unmatched pushes" and "unmatched pushes in a path of length N with final height H have positions i_d satisfying i_d parity = d mod2" maybe if there are no final elements (H=0), the condition is vacuous. We handle empty separately. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any hidden problem with "N" even and dp_even positive but the selected subsequence length even; if length even, first odd, last even. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp_even" if N even and dp_even is positive but the selected subsequence has first index odd and last even, but maybe the number of selected elements is even but not equal to final stack height because some selected elements could be below temp elements that are popped? No, selected elements are final stack; height equals count. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that a pop operation could remove a temp element that was pushed before a selected element? No, because selected element would be above it; cannot pop below selected. Our construction avoids. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "the sequence S, which is initially empty" and "Delete the last element of S. You cannot choose this operation if S is empty." If S has elements and we choose delete, we remove the last element; this could be a selected element if we don't plan. Our construction ensures no selected element is popped. Good.

Actually NEG+x < NEG, so no invalid update. But if x positive, invalid. Guard robust. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp_odd = max(dp_odd, x)" if x is NEG? A_i min -1e9, not NEG. Good.

We handle empty separately. Good.

No, selected elements are final stack; height equals count. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that a pop operation could remove a temp element that was pushed before a selected element? No, because selected element would be above it; cannot pop below selected. Our construction avoids. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "dp_odd = max(dp_odd, x)" if x is NEG? A_i min -1e9, not NEG. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that the answer for N odd should be dp_odd but if dp_odd is less than 0 and N odd, maybe we could choose to pop all elements and then have empty? No. Good.

We handle empty separately. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any hidden problem with "N" even and dp_even positive but the selected subsequence length even; if length even, first odd, last even. Good.

No, selected elements are final stack; height equals count. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that a pop operation could remove a temp element that was pushed before a selected element? No, because selected element would be above it; cannot pop below selected. Our construction avoids. Good.

No, selected elements are final stack; height equals count. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any possibility that a pop operation could remove a temp element that was pushed before a selected element? No, because selected element would be above it; cannot pop below selected. Our construction avoids. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any hidden problem with "the maximum possible value of the sum" if S can be empty and sum0, but if N even and dp_even positive
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    N = data[0]
    A = data[1:]

    NEG = -10**30
    dp_odd = NEG   # max sum of a valid subsequence ending at an odd index
    dp_even = NEG  # max sum of a valid subsequence ending at an even index

    for i, x in enumerate(A, 1):
        if i & 1:
            # Start a new subsequence here, or append to an even-indexed subsequence.
            if dp_even != NEG:
                dp_odd = max(dp_odd, dp_even + x)
            dp_odd = max(dp_odd, x)
        else:
            # Append to an odd-indexed subsequence.
            if dp_odd != NEG:
                dp_even = max(dp_even, dp_odd + x)

    if N & 1:
        ans = dp_odd
    else:
        ans = max(0, dp_even)

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
